Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Split Karatsuba algebra from its implementation, and the master theorem from binary search, so no body exceeds 893 words. New completion owners are unique IDs; original lessons remain archival.
- Added comments describing pending work, reversible mutation, path snapshots, mask selection, stable merging, half-result reuse, y-order preservation, tie counters, recurrence table updates and output meanings; all 32 lesson and 13 question Python ASTs remain unchanged.
- Converted inherited symbolic prose and recurrence-table entries into self-contained formula cards; mathematical products use conventional implicit multiplication with explicit grouping.
- Removed rounded exponents from asymptotic equalities/bounds: use exact log₂(3) or log₂(7), with decimal exponents only explanatory approximations.
- Changed the Karatsuba n-to-exponent illustration from an asserted digit-product count into an explicit growth-model expression; the 1000-digit expression rounds to 56,871.
- Separated the quicksort subproblem-size sum from its running-time function instead of asserting an exact triangular-number runtime.
- Corrected the binary-capacity bit-count formula to floor(log₂(W)) plus one, and carried update-rate units through the hypothetical time substitution.
- Qualified closest-pair strip sorting as a worst-case upper bound; equal tight local sorting costs support a tight recurrence, but an upper bound alone does not prove every actual input attains it.
- Distinguished exact cover from generalised exact cover, and libsolv from libmamba; Algorithm X need not choose the smallest column although that is a useful heuristic.
- Removed universal 8 MB C-stack sizing, instantaneous cache-hit claims, unsourced solver-size practicality and approximately-forty-item performance guarantees. Correct capacity/heap meanings from earlier verified modules are retained.
- Added precise search-state/CSP-domain/CSP-constraint terminology and actual dictionary probes; removed tail position as an alias for tail recursion because non-recursive calls can also be in tail position.
- Replaced process-oriented “content gap” notices with useful explanations of benchmark limits, instance-dependent MRV improvements and why canonicity does not establish monetary-design intent.
- Corrected the question suggesting a failed exchange attempt is usually a counterexample: a failed proof attempt alone does not disprove the greedy rule.

## dsa: Dynamic Programming

[Independent verification record](../content/dsa/08-dynamic-programming/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Removed the false literal one-step-per-state inequality for total basic work. The state-count upper bound is now separate from the O(n + 1) unit-cost Fibonacci bound.
- Replaced rough exponent limits with floors and a 1,041-state rectangular upper bound including the shared zero state; the actual target has 438 distinct reachable values, independently enumerated.
- Separated the no-valid-pair and valid-pair cases, so the final digit never relies on multiplying zero by an out-of-range state.
- Restricted both recursive rules to non-singleton intervals. Added a concrete acbac/cabca counterexample showing that an arbitrary maximum LCS may not be palindromic.
- Separated state counts with a started flag from digit-sum counts that need only tight; one symbol no longer changes dimension between formula lines.
- The dictionary now says Python raises ValueError without an explicit default, distinguishing a mathematical infinity convention from API behaviour.
- The dictionary no longer claims every request misses during a cold computation; only the first request per distinct key misses, and repeated recursive requests may already hit.
- The glossary now retains the plus-one band-width term and handles empty inputs directly.
- Qualified the minimum-duration statement by unlimited parallel resources and precedence-only constraints. Distinguished activities on nodes from activity durations on edges.
- The glossary now preserves exact-arithmetic associativity while warning that floating-point rounding can depend on the order.
- The glossary covers removing an endpoint or choosing a last action, rather than claiming every interval DP splits every interval and is necessarily cubic.
- Dictionary entries now count work per transition and avoid a universal twenty-item bitmask cutoff or hardware-independent complexity feasibility guarantees.
- The dictionary explicitly limits the shown table reductions to nonnegative integer inputs, includes parity/range checks and preserves distinct equal-valued positions.
- Moved remaining genuine algebraic recurrences, substitutions, inequalities and table-header products into standalone defined formula cards. Floor division in mathematical cards now uses a defined floor function; clear scalar products use implicit notation and grouping.
- Added local decode-state reasoning, word-break set/trie transitions, distance sanity bounds, interval application boundaries and last-project assignment state. Existing question ids, types, levels, answer keys and option counts remain unchanged.
- Replaced eight references to retired long-lesson numbers with the appropriate short-unit title or topic.
- All 37 originally tagged Python program ASTs are preserved. The previously unlabelled word-break Python fragment is explicitly tagged and fixture-executed. The tree traversal ellipsis is honestly labelled as a partial traversal rather than a completed DP.
- Removed four glossary source links that return 403; preserved the self-contained exercises and inherited naming confidence for independent review rather than inventing supporting primary URLs.
- Correct 19! from 120 quintillion to approximately 120 quadrillion; the numerical factorial itself was already correct.
- Repair two closing formula fences that had following prose on the same line, keeping that prose visible outside the cards.
- Validate every supplied coin denomination before equality-based deduplication. The prior order silently accepted [1, True] by merging the Boolean with integer one; three functions in two active fences are corrected and valid-domain results remain unchanged.
- Replace copied empty-route comments in grid and matrix-chain code with the correct zero-column and empty-product meanings.
- Make min/max function arguments and continuation-line grouping explicit in minimum-coins, matrix-chain, balloon and Held–Karp cards; use conventional unambiguous implicit scalar products.
- Define recurrence domains so separate zero/singleton/terminal bases take priority; add complete terminal cases and n/k definitions to the capped-transaction question.
- Replace inline symbolic assignments and inequalities with plain descriptions, genuine code or their already-defined formula cards; remove a fake uppercase-LCS program assignment.
- Distinguish actual Python range expressions and saved-diagonal prose from compressed invalid nested-for and pseudo-assignment text.
- Qualify word-break dictionary bounds for a nonempty dictionary and label slice/hash comparisons as growth proxies, since allocation and hashing change operation constants.
- Add zero as an explicit LIS candidate when no earlier element is smaller, avoiding the empty maximum.
- Include the zero-target column in the partition work bound, so an all-zero input does not appear to require zero work.
- Count threshold-band cells exactly, including the empty-prefix row and shorter end rows: the displayed 100,000-character example has 4,099,621 cells rather than claiming every row has forty-one.
- Use the full knapsack table in feedback for its introductory unit, before the later one-row backward-loop unit.
- Add local shared-row aliasing, capped-transaction and repeated grid/LCS-state explanations so questions do not require later units.
- Remove an unsupported universal speed ordering between memoisation and tabulation, and remove an unmeasured meet-in-the-middle timing comparison.
- Use a plus-one Myers distance factor for identical inputs and distinguish API-dependent diff choices from minimum scripts.
- Narrow the cache glossary: bare @lru_cache does not mean the unbounded @cache API; cold-cache body counts explicitly refer to the shown decorator, and instant execution claims are removed.
- Remove generic fixed-point-method aliases from the particular value-iteration algorithm; avoid linking previously populated memoisation answers to system-design cache warming.
- Qualify equivalent join reordering to legal inner joins and add the start-symbol restriction for Chomsky normal form with an empty-string production.
- Label Held–Karp and cubic dictionary numbers as growth proxies rather than executed step counts.
- Keep Bellman’s naming paragraph explicitly retrospective and add its quoted autobiographical provenance; retain medium historical confidence.
- Prove the exact unlimited coin-system claim using Kozen–Zaks’ finite counterexample bound, checked against an independent breadth-first shortest-coin oracle for every amount below three hundred.
- Remove two additional optional problem-site glossary source URLs that independently return 403, retaining self-contained exercise definitions and their medium provenance confidence.
- Clarify the matrix-cost takeaway as multiplications of individual numbers; scalar product can instead mean a complete vector dot product.
- Symbolic end-index arithmetic belongs in a formula card; consecutive zero-based numbering conveys the same precondition without an unnecessary formula.
- Clarify the grid endpoints as empty/full prefixes without introducing undefined string-length symbols in this unit.

Remaining gaps:

- All seven long lessons and their eighty questions are preserved as fourteen byte-identical archival files; all active question IDs, types, levels, answer keys and option counts remain unchanged.
- Full Hirschberg, CYK, Knuth-optimised BST, Manacher, Viterbi, RNA predictor, rerooting, FPTAS, assignment solver and branch-and-cut implementations remain conceptual or application context, matching the original scope rather than claiming new implementations.
- No physical-phone run, production runtime benchmark, C++ payload allocation experiment, PostgreSQL extension integration or live NumPy benchmark was performed; work expressions are labelled accordingly.
- Optional exercise-site naming provenance remains medium where access is blocked; required lesson/question links all load.
- Full app compilation, mobile layout, progress migration, PWA and publication integration belong to the coordinator after this scoped verification.

## dsa: Problem-Solving Patterns

[Independent verification record](../content/dsa/09-patterns/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Preserve eight archived lessons and 88 original question contracts while creating 42 coherent independently completable units and one focused tripled-bits question; add meaningful comments without changing any of the 44 inherited Python ASTs.
- Correct difference-array old/new notation, product and denominator grouping, overlap predicate comparison count, padded 1,441-slot timeline, heap singleton bounds, Held–Karp rectangular allocation and hash-partition consistency caveats.
- Growth expressions and logarithmic ratios are models rather than exact comparisons or speedups; remove remaining universal one-second/candidate-limit claims from standalone glossary examples.
- Explain the intentionally buggy insert-before-check and count-less quiz code accurately; preserve all executable statements and answer keys.
- Correct dictionary aliases for previous-smaller/greater and non-consecutive sequences. Replace remaining bare contiguous and half-open phrases that opened memory-layout or TCP-handshake definitions; use existing precise interval terms or explicit endpoint wording.
- Add a precise Histogram flush sentinel term and use it in the histogram unit; clarify final cur=0 rather than an out-of-bounds h[n] comparison.
- Heap tuple comparisons may use reflected greater-than; absence of __lt__ alone does not prove TypeError. Correct the standalone quiz wording.
- Explain IPO capital as an eligibility threshold, and make the file-flag example valid Python os.open with namespaced flags and umask caveat.
- Five inherited LeetCode identifiers now checked directly against official problem statements. Historical Kernighan/Wegner priority is explicitly reported indirectly and remains medium confidence.

Remaining gaps:

- No population-wide interview success/failure survey, generic runtime threshold, hardware throughput figure, physical-phone benchmark or production deployment measurement. Practical advice and numeric models are labelled accordingly.
- This is a patterns catalogue. Three sum, circular next greater, daily temperatures, weighted scheduling, Held–Karp, exact external frequency aggregation and sliding median are described as adaptations or covered in earlier modules rather than each receiving a complete production implementation.
- No turnkey approximate heavy-hitter/sketch recipe: candidate recovery and error/probability parameters are not specified.
- Direct Crow 1984 and Wegner 1960 originals were not retrieved; exact arithmetic, prefix constructions and bit algorithms were verified independently, and indirect historical claims remain medium.
- Whole-release generated dictionary, phone route rendering, offline update/migration and deployment checks are performed by the coordinating agent after this scoped verification.

Low-confidence areas:

- **medium** — pat-clarify-examples: Interview routine, suggested practice allocation, pattern hints and input-size table. Practical guidance and illustrative growth models, not a universal interview scoring rule, runtime threshold or throughput measurement; explicit caveats are retained.
- **medium** — pat-brute-to-lookup: Interview routine, suggested practice allocation, pattern hints and input-size table. Practical guidance and illustrative growth models, not a universal interview scoring rule, runtime threshold or throughput measurement; explicit caveats are retained.

- **medium** — pat-test-costs: Interview routine, suggested practice allocation, pattern hints and input-size table. Practical guidance and illustrative growth models, not a universal interview scoring rule, runtime threshold or throughput measurement; explicit caveats are retained.
- **medium** — pat-grid-prefix: Historical attribution of the summed-area table to Crow 1984. Read the original Viola–Jones 2001 paper, its four-reference construction and Crow 1984 bibliography. Direct Crow mirror retrieval was blocked; original Crow paper was not independently read in full.
- **medium** — pat-lowest-bit: Kernighan/Wegner historical attribution. The glossary now explicitly attributes the 1960 priority report to Sean Anderson citing Knuth; original Wegner paper and K&R exercise were not retrieved. The algorithm itself is independently verified.

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Explain loop termination without pretending this minimal copy handles all failures.
- Explain why n, rather than buffer capacity, is written.
- Interrupts and exceptions also enter the kernel; requests are the precise distinction.
- Kernel size or classification alone does not prove speed or safety.
- Explain the raw service request and output length.
- Distinguish generic libc syscall entry from raw assembly.
- Clarify address outputs without promising fixed values.
- Explain lifetime of observed heap storage.
- Randomisation depends on configuration and can repeat; not a guarantee of distinct addresses.
- Label environment-dependent procfs output honestly.
- Label vDSO address output as illustrative.
- Explain private-memory mutation.
- Explain why deterministic output ordering needs successful wait.
- Avoid treating PID namespace init or kernel boot tasks as identical to the user-process family model.
- Shared file description does not guarantee arbitrary message interleaving or atomic record writes.
- Explain non-returning success path.
- Explain the purpose of descriptor rebinding.
- Explain why conditional close preserves redirected stdout.
- Scope classic exec family rather than asserting every modern exec interface uses execve.
- Linux has distinct syscalls and newer clone3; containers are not only a clone flag.
- Clarify wait-status guard without moving statements.
- Distinguish concurrency from simultaneous execution.
- Label nondeterministic procfs counters.
- Explain the child half of each round trip.
- Explain the timer boundaries.
- Explain the seconds/nanoseconds conversion.
- No unmeasured universal dominance of indirect cost.
- KPTI is conditional and includes interrupts/exceptions, not only syscalls.
- Oversubscription harms are workload-dependent; one worker per core is not a universal server fix.
- No fixed per-message switch count across multicore configurations.
- Explain pipe lifetime ownership.
- Explain parent descriptor ownership.
- Explain signal-safe flag communication.
- Container shutdown defaults are configurable and Kubernetes grace period includes more than signal wait.
- Explain sizing versus mapping.
- Clarify message size and priority, independently execute receive ordering.
- Avoid implying Unix-specific socket features travel across a network.
- No unconditional fastest IPC ranking.
- ASLR can randomise supported stack, heap, shared-library and PIE executable mappings at launch, subject to configuration
- They share an address space, so the scheduler can avoid changing to a different memory map; useful TLB entries may remain
- When enabled, KPTI switches page tables at user/kernel transitions; PCID can avoid full TLB flushing on those switches
- It has been preempted far more often than it blocked voluntarily; CPU competition is one plausible cause
- The counters show many more preemptions than blocking switches over this task's lifetime, consistent with runnable CPU work. They do not reveal wait durations or prove a current bottleneck. Measure counter deltas, CPU utilisation, runnable queues, affinity and workload latency before deciding to add CPU resources.

These are cumulative event counts, not elapsed times. They suggest preemption but do not alone establish CPU saturation, the reason for preemption, or a current throughput problem; compare interval deltas and CPU/latency measurements.
- For many I/O-waiting connections, use an event-driven design with a measured, bounded worker pool
- Heavy runnable-thread oversubscription can harm locality and scheduling; measure a bounded worker pool and non-blocking I/O
- Spread the threads evenly over the cores:

```formula
r = N_threads / N_cores
r = 10,000 / 16 = 625
where
r: runnable threads per core
N_threads: busy threads, here 10,000
N_cores: CPU cores, here 16
In words: each core has about 625 threads taking turns on it.
```

That is under the simplified even-distribution assumption. This can cause scheduling contention and disrupt locality, but does not prove every cache is empty on every turn. Compare bounded worker pools, nonblocking I/O, CPU profiles and cache/scheduler counters. The appropriate worker count depends on blocking, CPU work and the service's shared data.

The ratio describes this deliberately busy workload; it is not a universal optimal thread count. CPU-heavy work, blocking operations, NUMA placement, priorities and runtime worker pools all affect the useful design.

## os: Threads & Scheduling

[Independent verification record](../content/os/02-threads-scheduling/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- It is called exponential because each update multiplies older contributions by the complement of the latest-burst weight. With the weight set to one half, the burst before last counts a quarter, the one before that an eighth, and so on. For a fixed weight strictly between zero and one, old contributions approach zero without reaching it in exact arithmetic. At a weight of one, only the latest burst counts; at zero, the prediction never updates.
- t_cpu: positive CPU-compute time per piece of work, in milliseconds (ms); 10 ms here (the estimate is undefined for zero compute time)
- a server that creates one thread per request without admission or concurrency limits has **no application-level bound**: a traffic spike can create thousands of threads until resource limits or failures intervene.
- A bounded **thread pool** reuses workers and caps active jobs; its submission queue still needs an overload policy.
- The preceding units explain thread state and runtime mappings. This unit uses the POSIX threads (**pthreads**) API on Linux and macOS to partition an array and combine completed results. Later units cover ownership, pools and Python's interpreter lock.
- As C runs, its vruntime rises. The current task is normally kept outside the queued-task tree, then reinserted at its new ordering position when descheduled. Classic CFS compares its service with queued competitors and applies slice/granularity and wake-up checks to decide when to reschedule; the picture shows key ordering rather than a node continuously moving through the tree.
- A higher weight makes vruntime grow *more slowly*, so it needs more real CPU service to reach the same virtual-clock advance. This explains the larger fair share under classic CFS and EEVDF; EEVDF additionally compares eligibility and virtual deadlines.
- N_eff: the group's quota-to-period capacity ceiling in CPUs' worth of service; it can be fractional and is not an affinity mask
- It returns the address of a local object whose lifetime ends on return, so dereferencing it later is undefined behaviour
- Correct. The automatic result object dies when compute returns. Retained or cached stack storage does not extend its lifetime; a later dereference can fail in arbitrary ways.
- Correct. Some thread resources and the return value remain available for a later join. Automatic local objects have already ended their lifetimes; retained stack storage does not keep them alive.
- Returning from main ends the process and all its threads. In an ordinary run main returns while the worker is sleeping, so nothing is printed. This is the likely result, rather than a timing guarantee: if main is descheduled long enough after creation, the worker could print before main resumes. Check pthread_create for failure and join a successfully created thread before returning, or call pthread_exit(NULL) to end only the main thread.
- False sharing: the counters occupy one cache line, so alternating writes from different cores can transfer its ownership
- Correct. This CPU loop neither returns nor suspends, so the event loop cannot resume its other tasks. An await only yields if it actually suspends; returning also gives control back.
- A short I/O-bound burst that becomes ready during the long FCFS burst waits until that burst finishes. Until it runs and issues its next read, the disk may be idle. Several waiting jobs can then issue reads in a cluster. Exact CPU and disk utilisation needs the omitted disk-service times. Under an ideal round-robin model with a 10 ms quantum, short bursts can run after the current quantum and other queued bursts, rather than after a whole 100 ms CPU burst. This can reduce the convoy delay; no fixed throughput gain or disk-idle fraction follows from the supplied numbers alone.
- Nice values run from −20 to 19. Lower nice means greater fair-scheduling weight, so the nice −5 task receives more long-run CPU service than the nice 10 task under the specified same-group contention. This is weighted sharing rather than strict preemption: the nice 10 task still receives a share. Linux real-time priorities use the opposite numeric direction and a different scheduling class.
- Two continuously CPU-bound tasks compete alone in the same fair group on one CPU, without quotas or higher-class work. Both start at nice 0 and get 50%. You successfully renice one to nice 1 (weight 820). Roughly what shares do they now get?
- Weight sets long-run share under same-group sustained contention; equal nice weights receive equal shares when no other constraints intervene. A slice affects virtual deadlines. For fresh requests at equal virtual runtimes:

```formula
d = v + (1024r) / w
d_audio = v + 1 ms
d_hog = v + 3 ms
where
d: a fresh request's virtual deadline, in milliseconds (ms)
v: its starting virtual runtime, in ms; equal for these two requests
r: its requested slice, in ms
w: its weight, 1024 for both nice 0 tasks
d_audio, d_hog: deadlines of the fresh audio and CPU-heavy requests, in ms
ms: milliseconds, thousandths of a second
In words: at equal starting virtual runtimes the shorter fresh request has the earlier virtual deadline.
```

The audio request therefore has the earlier deadline in that comparison. At later selections, virtual runtimes, eligibility, request progress and preemption protection can differ; an arbitrary audio deadline is not always earlier. A shorter slice does not guarantee a wall-clock latency or change the scheduling class.
- A 500m CPU request contributes a half-CPU reservation for placement and proportional shares under contention; achieved service depends on the competing hierarchy and capacity.
- With local policy, enough node-0 memory and no later migration, a main thread on node 0 can place a newly written private array there.
- Pinning restricts this thread; separately exclude competing work and inspect SMT siblings when seeking a dedicated core.
- An eight-CPU root domain with a configured 95% total deadline-bandwidth limit has 7.6 CPUs of reservation capacity before accounting for fair-server reservations.
- A measured p99 of 79 ms marks a high quantile; ties, interpolation and sample size affect how many observations are at or beyond it.
- A task that ran 0.2 ms ago may be treated as cache-hot under the checked 0.5 ms threshold; other migration conditions still matter.
- With max_workers=10, at most ten pool jobs execute at once; idle workers are reused and fewer may be needed.
- {"glossary":"base-slice","field":"aliases","before":["default slice","custom slice","shorter slice","requested slice","requested slices"],"after":["default slice"]}
- For equal starting virtual runtimes and fresh requests, a nice 0 task's 1 ms slice has an earlier deadline than a 3 ms request.
- For equally weighted eligible fresh requests starting at the same virtual runtime, a 1 ms slice has an earlier deadline than a 3 ms slice.
- A fresh nice 0 request starting at virtual runtime 10 ms with a 0.5 ms slice has virtual deadline 10.5 ms.
- A monotonic per-run-queue virtual-runtime reference in Linux's fair scheduler. Classic CFS places sleepers relative to it; EEVDF adds lag-aware placement. It is maintained as a floor from tracked entities rather than always equalling the smallest current queued value.
- Classic CFS's minimum-granularity target used when choosing scheduling periods and service slices. It helps avoid excessively small slices as runnable task counts grow; weights and other preemption conditions still affect actual execution.
- A balanced tree keeps path lengths logarithmic in its number of nodes; the exact comparisons and rotations depend on its shape and operation.
- A count tracking references to an object. In CPython it participates in object lifetime management, alongside cycle collection; immortal objects and free-threaded implementations require additional rules. A simple increment/decrement model does not describe every object or guarantee immediate destruction.
- For an ordinary non-immortal CPython object, adding a strong reference normally increases its reference count.
- An additional Python interpreter inside one process, with separate interpreter state. Python 3.14's InterpreterPoolExecutor uses isolated interpreters with their own GILs, enabling independent bytecode execution on several cores. Historically, not every subinterpreter configuration had a separate GIL.
- A cgroup CPU-time budget shared across its fair-class tasks and CPUs over an accounting period. Exhausted local runtime allocations throttle affected queues. Ancestor budgets, per-CPU accounting and burst credit can change the exact timing; a perfectly simultaneous group-wide stop is an ideal model.
- A locality domain for memory allocation and CPU access. A NUMA node may contain CPUs, memory or both; memoryless and CPU-less nodes exist. Relative access cost depends on the hardware topology.
- The final hardware cache level before accesses proceed beyond the cache hierarchy. It is often an L3 shared by a group of cores, but level, size and sharing vary by CPU design.
- The fair scheduler's imaginary reference that serves runnable competitors simultaneously in proportion to their scheduling weights. Equal weights give equal shares; unequal weights do not.
- Hierarchical fair scheduling that divides contended CPU service among sibling task groups by group weight, then among their members. Groups can be nested beyond two levels; quotas and topology also constrain service.
- Sending a large ordinary array through a process pool can incur serialization and copying; shared-memory designs can avoid moving the full payload.
- one CPU burst, defined in the next unit
- In the preceding feedback-trace unit, B uses 2 ms per burst.

## os: Memory Management

[Independent verification record](../content/os/05-memory/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Original8086 standalone card now keeps only twenty address bits, including overflow boundaries.
- Dense leaf-table formula now rounds up whole pages under explicit alignment.
- Minor-fault question uses actual backing/cache evidence instead of inferring residency from read history.
- Observed first-touch543 counter distinguished from explicit16384/32 models.
- NMT definition includes tracked Java heap and states untracked allocation gaps.
- General mmap/demand-zero/in-process-cache aliases resolve the correct meaning; OPT ties, PCID retention and LSan availability qualified.

Remaining gaps:

- All seven original topics, fourteen archival files and eighty-four original question contracts are retained. No substantive topic was intentionally removed.
- No privileged CR2/CR3/page-table manipulation, real CPU TLB/PMU benchmark, live OOM/cgroup-pressure, swap, production Kubernetes, Redis or alternative-allocator stress experiment was performed.
- Native checks use Linux x86-64 userspace under emulation on an ARM64 Docker host, glibc2.39,512MiB/oneCPU bounds and20-second program timeouts. These verify selected behaviour, not physical-hardware latency or a universal minor-fault count.
- perf, dmesg, proc counters and logs shown in lessons are explicitly illustrative. Capacity, weighted-cost, page and allocation arithmetic use stated assumptions rather than undocumented measurements.
- The independent verifier reviewed all active text/questions/definitions and fetched every active source URL. Evidence is bounded source/model/fixture checking, not external proof of every possible implementation or workload.
- Phone rendering, whole-course compilation, saved-date migration and publication remain the coordinating agent’s integration work; this verifier does not claim to have completed them.

Low-confidence areas:

- **medium** — mem-large-page-mappings: Common macOS Apple-silicon page granule. Apple instructs querying the running page size. This host reports 16384; that is one configuration, not proof of every Apple deployment.
- **medium** — mem-first-touch: Observed first-touch minor-fault count. The separately executed emulated Linux x86-64/glibc2.39 example reported 543, while 16384 and32 are explicit one-fault-per-base/huge-page arithmetic models. No claim that this runtime reproduced those exact model counters.
- **medium** — mem-tlb-coherence: PCID retention, shootdowns and kernel-entry overhead. Intel invalidation rules and Linux documentation checked; implementations may discard additional entries. No privileged hardware experiment or workload speedup benchmark was run.
- **medium** — mem-huge-page-policy: THP allocation, compaction, COW and application performance. Version/configuration-specific primary documentation checked; no host policy changes, Redis load or actual large-page latency benchmark.
- **medium** — mem-linux-reclaim: Reclaim admission, promotion and generation behaviour. Primary Linux source/docs checked with version and path exceptions; no live memory-pressure, swap or MGLRU stress experiment.
- **medium** — mem-glibc-retention: Allocator retention and alternative allocator comparisons. glibc2.39 source and native chunk fixture checked; RSS, contention, allocation lifetimes and alternative allocator performance remain workload-dependent without comparative benchmarks.
- **medium** — mem-container-budgets: Kubernetes/runtime/container policy. Primary documentation checked; exact behaviour depends on deployed runtime, cgroup mode, Kubernetes features and flags. No production OOM, JVM/Go/Node memory-pressure deployment was exercised.
- **medium** — glossary: Inherited medium-confidence generic and product/configuration definitions. Existing medium ratings retained except Denning, whose original paper is now fetched. Additional time-sensitive platform/allocator entries remain medium; no low-confidence entries remain.

## os: File Systems & Storage

[Independent verification record](../content/os/06-file-systems/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Wrong RAID reconstruction example referred to a failed disk1 but reconstructed the different final data chunk; now the surviving chunks reconstruct disk1 correctly.
- Ext4 htree lookup no longer promises a generic logarithmic worst-case bound; hash-leaf scanning and collisions are explained.
- Current PostgreSQL discussion qualifies normal buffered I/O with its developer-only direct-I/O option and measurement-based buffer sizing.
- NVMe differentiates 65,536 queue slots from 65,535 outstanding commands; the standard’s stated outstanding-command bound is sourced.
- Fast symlink lookup avoids a separate target-payload read, not every inode or path I/O.
- Globally ambiguous dirty-data, storage-cache-hit and NFS-handle links resolve to file-storage definitions; actual compiled ID, definition and rendered button checks pass.
- Snapshot, NOCOW/nodatasum, FUA, reflink, RAID-write-cost and filesystem-checker definitions lose unsupported unconditional guarantees; valid original definitions remain unchanged.
- Writeback-error reporting is separated from preserving failed dirty bytes; a successful fsync retry is not recovery.
- NFS retries distinguish non-idempotent effects from changed replies, and replay caches are scoped to retained protocol state.
- GFS at-least-once guarantee is explicitly for successful appends, and original-paper chunk-size notation is retained instead of implying an independently established byte convention.
- Code comments explain remaining bytes, shared offsets, payload mapping, output meaning and durable publication; all original executable ASTs remain unchanged.
- Fraction denominators, exponents and unit conversions are explicitly grouped; clear scalar products use ordinary implicit algebra. Numeric assumptions remain labeled models rather than benchmark observations.
- Dentry cache and payload page cache are different caches; VFS documentation.
- Read-only open does not replace prior contents.
- Typographical duplicate.
- Dirty thresholds are not hard universal backlog guarantees.
- Pointer payload excludes interior pointer blocks and final-block rounding.
- Distinguish computed pointer payload from allocated metadata.
- Preserve conventional product/acronym capitalisation.
- Preserve conventional product/acronym capitalisation.
- Preserve conventional product/acronym capitalisation.
- Preserve conventional product/acronym capitalisation.
- Preserve conventional product/acronym capitalisation.
- Preserve conventional product/acronym capitalisation.
- Closing code fence must occupy its own line; prose otherwise swallowed.
- Closing code fence must occupy its own line; prose otherwise swallowed.
- Remove atomic-sector simplification and misleading no-recovery implication from answer explanations.
- Payload cache hit does not promise no device I/O; avoid universal latency ratio and shared-host cache operation advice.
- Little’s law describes an average; NVMe does not guarantee one hardware queue per CPU.
- Traffic amplification alone does not establish exact physical wear or lifetime.
- Match lesson’s device-specific programming caveat.
- Restore spaces between product and version.

It does not write dirty data, so run sync first.', 'example': 'sync; echo 3 > /proc/sys/vm/drop_caches empties the clean caches on a test box.', 'source': 'https://docs.kernel.org/admin-guide/sysctl/vm.html'}, 'after': {'id': 'drop-caches', 'term': 'drop_caches', 'aliases': ['/proc/sys/vm/drop_caches'], 'definition': 'A Linux control that attempts to reclaim clean page-cache and/or reclaimable slab objects such as dentries and inodes. ## os: I/O & Event-Driven Servers

[Independent verification record](../content/os/07-io/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Removed the universal default-seccomp claim: supported Docker setups ordinarily apply a default profile, whereas unset Kubernetes workload profiles can be Unconfined when seccompDefault is disabled. Confirmed both primary documentation pages and all three contextual aliases.
- Independent pass: 66 correction locations; full before/after evidence /tmp/os-io-short-verify/corrections.json.
- Distinguished operation contracts from API names: kqueue EVFILT_AIO can report completion, while EVFILT_READ is readiness; io_uring poll requests do not automatically make a loop a proactor. Rewrote the proactor question with explicit event contracts, keeping its original key.
- Narrowed zero-syscall SQPOLL claims to active-poller/shared-CQ collection and qualified speculative-mitigation overhead.
- Corrected stream EOF versus zero-size datagrams, Python sendall result behavior, SIGHUP hangup/reload meaning, EINTR retry guidance, O_NONBLOCK storage waits, PREEMPT_RT spinlock distinctions, NVMe fabric transports, and safe device-assignment prerequisites.
- Removed guarantees about VLAN-tag packet-rate changes, fixed resource/NUMA/TX completion assumptions, and described shared-nothing application ownership without claiming a lockless entire runtime.

Remaining gaps:

- No course topic was intentionally omitted. Original summary-only evidence-gap callouts were carried into the split lessons rather than dropped.
- No live device/IRQ/DMA driver, NIC configuration change, DPDK deployment, nginx/Redis deployment or production throughput benchmark.
- Existing container security policy rejects io_uring setup with EPERM; compiled request code is not claimed to have executed an actual request. Restrictions were not lifted.
- The dangling-stack-buffer question is intentionally unsafe when a receive remains pending; compiled only, without executing memory corruption.
- XDP compiled to a BPF object and passed 52 native-C synthetic cases, but real BPF verifier acceptance and live attachment are not proven. Kernel IRQ/MMIO behavior uses a clearly identified fixture rather than a loaded driver.
- Original archival Infineon URL currently responds 403; original archives cannot be rewritten. Active lessons have working references.
- No application-deployment, NIC-driver, arbitrary concurrency or throughput proof follows from finite fixtures. 326,432 synthetic XDP cases are host-C tests; BPF compilation does not establish verifier load/attach acceptance.
- Glossary confidence is conservative: inherited secondary/historical definitions and release/device-sensitive cases remain medium; no sentence-by-sentence proof of every conceivable mode combination is claimed.

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- maxLength applies to a particular validated record; another covering record can authorise the route. NotFound uses validated records, and the AS path supplies a claimed origin rather than authenticated history.
- Replaced the misleading independent-lost-KEEPALIVE model with accepted UPDATE/KEEPALIVE receipt, the one-third scheduling recommendation and TCP delivery/retransmission limitations.
- Qualified RIP’s ordinary one-cost links and OSPF DR/BDR adjacency-count domain, compatibility and convergence assumptions. Reverse-path checks do not authenticate every source or universally prevent spoofing.
- Removed actual HTTP-field, cryptographic-integrity, CPU-word-size, TLS-proxy, software-service-layer, TCP-listening-socket, TCP/QUIC-PTO, register-allocation, SMTP reverse-path, unit-operation-cost, split-DNS, canary-traffic, Agile-WIP, nanosecond, global-TLB-page and IPsec NAT-T matches from active prose and questions. Added precise application NAT traversal and routing link cost concepts.
- An observed transport address need not be globally reachable or usable by a different peer.
- Explained IPv6 Next Header extension headers and ICMPv4/ICMPv6 numbers, the zero-exponent power-of-two case and the /32 exception to the interesting-octet shortcut. Removed unsourced unicast prevalence.
- Preserved and checked RFC 6691 MSS/option distinctions, ID-wrap dimensions, 4-to-3 modulo assumptions, scoped OSPF flooding, DHCP cookie/BOOTP overlap, qualified defaults/traversal/DAD/ARP guarantees, standalone prerequisites and explanatory code comments.
- Earlier definition implied all NAT gateways use public Internet addresses; now distinguishes AWS public Internet-gateway paths from private NAT gateways without Elastic IP addresses, including private transit-gateway routing.

Remaining gaps:

- No live BGP announcements, rogue DHCP injection, host route changes, firewall changes or physical-router experiments were performed. No protocol convergence, forwarding latency, current market share or Internet adoption benchmark is claimed.
- The Python examples are bounded teaching models, not complete parsers, routers, NATs, OSPF speakers or DHCP clients. Their omissions are retained beside the code.
- The illustrative Linux ip-route/ip-rule mutation commands were source-checked rather than executed against a full matching topology; this remains a concrete execution gap for the verifier.
- Author phone verification uses exact app CSS, Markdown renderer, glossary compiler section and diagram compiler in an isolated fixture. Full production lesson/quiz routes, progress migration, PWA update and offline checks remain coordinating integration work after independent verification.
- Inherited omitted-after-review notes remain explicit: no unsupported universal OS defaults, pricing/allocation timeline, route/adoption counts, timing guarantees, client-mode prevalence or incident financial-loss total was reintroduced.
- The standalone OSPF cost model does not model platform maximum-cost handling or every vendor rounding policy; its examples stay within the specified positive 16-bit interface-cost range.
- All 435 glossary definitions, examples and aliases were read. The actual compiled renderer was separately inspected for opposite meanings; precise application NAT traversal and routing link cost were added rather than changing other modules’ aliases.
- Primary-source review uses relevant passages, not a claim to have read entire protocol specifications or to have independently reproduced historical Internet outages.

## networks: Transport Layer: TCP & UDP in Depth

[Independent verification record](../content/networks/03-tcp/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- A reset-guess probability example incorrectly treated the expected 65,536 uniform attempts as guaranteed coverage. It now states the first-hit expectation and explicitly denies that guarantee.
- AccECN no longer says original DCTCP requires it: RFC 8257 uses modified ECE feedback independently of RFC 9768 AccECN. The glossary distinguishes those mechanisms.
- SYN_SENT and SYN_RCVD definitions now include the RFC-required simultaneous-open path rather than restricting them to client-waits-SYNACK/server states.
- Python daemon threads do not end merely because the main thread exits: process exit waits until no non-daemon threads remain. The glossary now gives that rule and abrupt-cleanup limitation.
- The old select(10,000 sockets) example violated the usual Linux fd_set/FD_SETSIZE limit. It now distinguishes select’s normal 1,024 descriptor limit from poll’s larger scanned set.
- Application-limited and loss-based entries/examples no longer make universal window-growth or overflow claims; Reno/sawtooth examples distinguish fast-recovery events from RTO behaviour.
- QUIC path validation is distinguished from handshake/token address validation; packet-number-space aliases now open a definition that explains the separate numbering/ACK context.
- recvfrom/sendto definitions now scope datagram guarantees to UDP rather than claiming these APIs are datagram-only; BlockingIOError includes connection-in-progress error codes.
- Physical denominators include all necessary brackets, UDP bit/byte conversion is explicit, window-scaling worked substitutions retain units, and binary floating-point output is labelled rounded.
- Code comments are combined into readable purpose-oriented lines without changing executable statements; obsolete numbered cross-references now point to the correct short unit.

Remaining gaps:

- No WAN packet-capture experiment, SYN flood, AQM benchmark or QUIC migration experiment. Source-supported algorithms and explicit bounded models do not imply measured deployment performance.
- The selector loop deliberately retains partial-write/would-block defects for its own debugging question; it is not a production server. Services need total deadlines and resource/connection policies.
- Advanced UDP options and implementation-specific recovery/state handling are described selectively rather than implementing a full transport stack.
- The Chiu/Jain scanned first page/abstract was inspected visually, not a complete independent proof of the paper; the fairness attribution remains limited to its simplified model.

## networks: Application-Layer Protocols

[Independent verification record](../content/networks/04-application/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Receiver DMARC pass still requires either aligned result, but RFC 9989 section 8 requires fully participating domain owners to send with both aligned SPF and DKIM and forbids relying solely on SPF with p=reject. Added this distinction to the lesson, glossary and rollout question, preserving the original answer index.
- Clarified that gRPC framing is independent of protobuf serialization, and protobuf tags are varint-encoded integers rather than invariably one byte.
- Corrected HPACK Huffman coding to optional, HTTP/2 versus HTTP/3 field-block framing, and Connection: close to close after the response rather than prematurely after a request.
- Corrected owned glossary entries for publicly derivable QUIC Initial protection, handshake/token address validation, Karn timestamp exceptions and conditional spin-bit observation.
- Qualified EOF to positive-size ordinary TCP reads, moved the bounce-address alias from notification-message to envelope sender, and qualified MX fallback and equal-preference selection.
- Eliminated remaining bare email-context SPF wording, including formula definitions, so the actual dictionary opens Email SPF rather than shortest path first.
- Qualified release-specific SSH/TOTP/NTP examples, cryptographic performance, NAT reachability, late-media usefulness and PLI/keyframe behaviour; retained historical Kademlia uptime rationale as source-specific.

Remaining gaps:

- Cookie revision is a live draft; browser enforcement, partitioning/eviction and restore behaviour remain implementation/policy dependent.
- No production HTTP parser or current browser cookie matrix is claimed.
- HTTP/2 and SFTP throughput examples are ideal feedback models, not measured benchmarks.
- No live QUIC migration/load-balancer or HTTP/2 reset-exhaustion experiment; research attack rate is attributed historical reporting.
- No resolver or DNSSEC validator interoperability lab; the ASCII encoder is explicitly restricted.
- No present-day resolver entropy, DNSSEC prevalence, KeyTrap CPU duration or browser/device policy matrix.
- Kademlia PDF required visual reading separately; SAD DNS text was extracted through native PDFKit and its rate-limit passages read.
- Local STARTTLS SMTP exercises certificate/hostname checking, AUTH and actual message/envelope serialisation, but no external provider delivery is claimed.
- Provider bulk policies and RFC 9989 deployment compatibility are time-sensitive; no rollout duration or inbox-delivery guarantee.
- No clock-accuracy measurement, NTS exchange or SSH/SFTP interoperability session; wire dialogues are labelled abbreviated protocol fixtures.
- OpenSSH defaults are release-specific, PTP accuracy requires engineered deployment, and gRPC library defaults/propagation/receive limits differ.
- Kademlia original PDF has broken text extraction; native PDFKit rendered pages 2–6 and those images were read for metric, state, uptake assumptions and lookup. No every-page read is claimed.
- No torrent swarm, DHT convergence, NAT traversal or WebRTC media benchmark; finite consistency checks are not live protocol proofs.
- No current DHT population/typical rounds/TURN share or universal interactive-latency limit.
- Receiver DMARC pass and RFC 9989 Domain Owner full-participation requirements are now distinguished; existing deployments based on RFC 7489 can differ.
- Secondary glossary references are retained for background concepts; their entries are scored medium rather than presented as fresh deployment measurements.

Low-confidence areas:

- **medium** — app-cookie-scope-and-lifetime: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Cookie revision and browser storage behaviour are draft/policy dependent.
- **medium** — app-cookie-cross-site-and-cost: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Cookie draft, prefixes, implementation policies and encoder choices can change.
- **medium** — app-h2-credit-and-shutdown: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Rapid Reset and Chrome removal are attributed historical/product claims; throughput is an ideal feedback model.
- **medium** — app-quic-migration-and-discovery: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Actual migration, routing and fallback require coordinated deployment; no live migration lab.
- **medium** — app-dns-poisoning-defences: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Case preservation and side-channel feasibility are implementation-specific; historical research is attributed.
- **medium** — app-dnssec-validation-chain: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Standards checked; KeyTrap behaviour and operational rollover failures depend on implementation and state.
- **medium** — app-dns-encrypted-transports: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Browser/device encrypted-DNS policies are version- and configuration-dependent.
- **medium** — app-email-dmarc-alignment: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. New May 2026 RFC conformance requirements checked; deployment compatibility and provider policy remain time-sensitive.
- **medium** — app-email-framing-and-tls-policy: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Smuggling outcome depends on the vulnerable parser pair and policy; no external relay experiment.
- **medium** — app-ntp-asymmetry-and-clocks: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Clock discipline, chrony/ntpd options and future UTC policy are source-specific.
- **medium** — app-ntp-wire-and-authentication: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Wire fields verified; no clock-accuracy, NTS or PTP interoperability measurement.
- **medium** — app-ssh-transport-and-authentication: Source-specific operational, product or deployment details. Release-specific OpenSSH defaults and affected Terrapin modes are source-specific; no SSH interoperability session.

## networks: Network Security

[Independent verification record](../content/networks/05-network-security/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Fixed WPA2 PMK scope in the lesson and standalone question card: it derives pairwise keys; the AP supplies group key material through a separate hierarchy. NIST SP 800-97 explicitly distinguishes PMK/PTK from GMK/GTK.
- Removed ticket nonce from the opaque session-ticket alias and added a precise TLS ticket nonce entry. RFC 9846 defines separate ticket and ticket_nonce fields; the latter derives a per-ticket resumption PSK.
- Corrected flood-and-learn: eligible received frames supply source-address learning; unknown destination flooding is a separate operation and learning does not require a reply.
- Qualified ESP worked-example protection to the negotiated confidentiality-and-integrity configuration, and included cookie-only HelloRetryRequest in the TLS explanation.
- Inspected 3,413 real compiled dictionary contexts and narrowed wording/aliases to eliminate 14 concrete unrelated meanings, including CPU instruction windows versus replay width, QUIC packet numbers versus CCMP counters, video frame rates versus Wi-Fi traffic, KEKs versus WPA2 pairwise secrets, leaf switches versus leaf certificates, and C++ control blocks versus a filtering control.
- Replaced symbolic CRC/replay-window prose with natural-language operations outside the already fully defined formula cards, and corrected a retired long-lesson ordinal reference.
- Independently confirmed the newly published RFC 9846 is the July 2026 TLS 1.3 revision, superseding RFC 8446. The historical RFC 8446 cipher-suite statement remains explicitly historical.
- Closed the separately attributed earlier-owner IP NAT-gateway follow-up against fetched AWS documentation; the compiled Security dictionary now opens the correct public/private gateway definition.

Remaining gaps:

- No new substantive course topic was dropped. Existing disclosed gaps remain: universal attack success/provider capacities, complete Target forensics, live endpoint chain guarantees, trusted-root/adoption counts, vendor exploit-frequency comparisons, universal hardware guessing rates, WEP exact deprecation/test-vector attribution, industry dwell/retention and detector precision rankings are not supplied without comparable evidence.
- No live kernel nftables-policy application was performed: this disposable environment returned netlink Operation not permitted. No permission was lifted and no retry bypass was attempted.
- WireGuard/IPsec/enterprise Wi-Fi deployments were checked against primary specifications; no live VPN, radio, RADIUS or physical-NIC deployment is claimed.
- Synthetic packet fixtures check tool semantics and one example signature, not production detection coverage or malicious intent.
- No formal cryptographic security proof, unbounded implementation correctness or real throughput guarantee is claimed.

## networks: Wireless, Mobile & Modern Networks

[Independent verification record](../content/networks/06-wireless-modern/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- The radio-state diagram still equated connected with fast/high power and idle with no radio resources. It now shows RRC context and DRX on an explicitly simplified setup/release path, consistent with the prose.
- CTS duration now explicitly means the remaining protected exchange rather than an unchanged RTS duration.
- The modulo-remapping explanation now states removal of the last-labelled member before using an equal-remainder proof; the question and physical-label model agree.
- Scheduling-request aliases incorrectly resolved to the grant assigning resources. A separate sourced Scheduling request entry now distinguishes asking from receiving permission.
- Multi-Link Operation no longer requires links in different bands; modes and hardware constrain concurrency.
- SIFS was called the shortest gap in all Wi-Fi. It is now defined by immediate-reply use and its relation to DIFS under the supplied PHY example.
- MPDU omitted its frame check sequence. Its definition now includes header, any body and FCS.
- Combined OFDMA/MU-MIMO, eNodeB/gNodeB and S-GW+P-GW aliases no longer collapse different mechanisms or components into one definition; PDU no longer aliases an entire PDU session.
- Virtualisation is defined as resource abstraction with NFV as an example; NSA wording is scoped to common option 3 rather than all non-standalone architectures.
- Tail time no longer aliases tail energy: a duration is not an energy measurement.
- Leaf-spine definitions now state the fully connected different-leaf model and distinguish equal topology from configured equal routing cost.
- Non-blocking now distinguishes strict circuit non-blocking from rearrangeable and packet-fabric claims.
- Switch buffers and microbursts no longer guarantee that every shallow buffer overflows or every deep buffer creates delay.
- A VLAN identifier is 12 bits within the tag; the whole IEEE 802.1Q tag is not a 12-bit tag.
- Generic P4 match-action processing no longer resolves as an OpenFlow-specific highest-priority table; a distinct generic entry covers the abstraction.
- OpenFlow fast failover selects the first live action bucket, whose watch can involve ports or groups, rather than simply the first live port.
- ICMPv6 Packet Too Big and MTU black-hole definitions now distinguish classic error-based discovery from possible packetization-layer recovery.
- VPN no longer universally means an encrypted tunnel; private logical connectivity and cryptographic confidentiality are distinct.
- RACK-TLP now says a tail-loss probe may send new data or retransmit the highest-sequence segment, rather than always retransmitting the last segment.
- TLS round-trip claims now describe ordinary full handshakes and account for resumption, early data and retries.
- Hosts-file resolution order is configurable; DNSSEC authenticates validated data and denial rather than encrypting or making all data correct.
- BGP hijack wording now concerns authorisation rather than mere ownership of a prefix.
- Unrepeatered no longer aliases an optical amplifier; generic DC no longer aliases submarine high-voltage shore feeding.
- Fibre signal-envelope delay now explicitly uses effective group index rather than generic phase refractive index; the unchanged 1.47 numerical model is supported by Corning’s dated 1.4682 characterisation.
- An isotropic antenna is now a separate ideal reference concept rather than an alias for antenna gain.
- Ground-station and bent-pipe definitions no longer require large antennas, public Internet service or a geographically nearby user gateway.
- Renumbering now allows staged old/new addresses instead of claiming every device and dependency must change simultaneously.
- Local formula indices are defined explicitly, inline modulo pseudocode is natural-language wording, and acronym capitalisation is repaired in short-unit introductions.

Remaining gaps:

- No content topic deliberately removed; naturally small coherent units are below400 words and retain 3-minute estimates for diagrams, calculations and careful examples rather than added filler.
- No RF, cellular core, physical fabric, live cloud, orbital-link or production-incident benchmark.
- No complete release-specific channel/band table, advanced inactive-state procedure, arbitrary traffic-matrix proof or exhaustive network-update theorem implementation.
- No RF, modem, physical switch, cloud account or orbital link was deployed during independent review. Primary specifications/implementations and bounded models support the mechanisms; native execution covers the five Python examples and trusted loopback TLS only.
- Peripheral glossary descriptions and some historical/commercial shorthand retain medium confidence where fetched primary source families corroborate the core concept but do not establish every optional implementation detail or commercial classification. No low-confidence core teaching claim remains.

Low-confidence areas:

- **medium** — wm-wifi-generations: Generation capabilities, regulatory spectrum and equipment behaviour. Primary Alliance/Ofcom/ns-3 evidence checked; capabilities and permissions remain release, device and location dependent, and no RF or interoperability conformance experiment was performed.
- **medium** — wm-cellular-performance-edge: Radio energy, mobility, slicing and edge deployment outcomes. 3GPP Release17, ITU and platform guidance support the mechanisms; no operator/modem measurements or end-to-end guarantees are claimed.
- **medium** — wm-fabric-queues-dctcp: Fabric queues, recovery defaults and current cloud/network operational behaviour. Current primary implementation/vendor documents checked; hardware policies, defaults and product features are time-sensitive. All numerical capacity and queue examples are conditional models.
- **medium** — wm-internet-commercial-routing: Commercial tiers, routing incidents and policy models. Original Gao/Rexford, RFCs and operator incident reports checked. Commercial classification and the simplified export policy are not universal protocol requirements.

## architecture: Digital Logic

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Fixed the lesson and original subtraction question: retained four-bit arithmetic is reduced modulo sixteen; an unreduced eighteen is no longer equated with two, nor fifteen with negative one.
- Fixed three adder cards whose plus sign was wrongly defined as Boolean OR; their operands now use ordinary binary addition.
- Bracketed both quiz timing substitutions before the picosecond unit, keeping the full arithmetic sum within that unit.
- Replaced 113 generic formula-card readings with readings of the actual expression or proposed identity. Repaired sentences, bullet emphasis and punctuation split around mathematical cards; removed mathematical multi-bit products disguised as inline code.
- Clarified Brent–Kung counting: eleven scheduled rows in the original buffered sixty-four-bit layout include forwarding; the longest unbuffered path contains ten combining cells. Independently checked the graph.
- Qualified same-phase latch versus flip-flop shifting, asynchronous-reset deassertion, synchroniser reliability and statement-specific non-blocking scheduling; no fixed MTBF or universal hardware guarantee is implied.
- Corrected a maxterm distractor label to row one while retaining the original answer key; the expression is still a distractor for the minterm question.
- Clarified that unused instruction encodings are not automatically allowed don’t-cares: required illegal-instruction behaviour must still be implemented.
- Corrected inherited dictionary meanings for active-low assertion, active-high decoder assumptions, general latches, combinational settling, feedback, hardware counters, programme-counter instruction address and finite-width two’s-complement negation.
- Narrowed ordinary consensus and latch-transparent/opaque aliases; open-drain now reuses the existing output definition rather than opening wired-OR wiring. Added a precise open-collector output entry.
- Qualified activity factors, capacitive versus other dynamic power, drive strength, multiported-register costs, row-buffer latency, FPGA configuration, VHDL/Verilog event sensitivity and topology assumptions.
- Used the independently fetched original Shannon paper’s confirmed 1938 publication date rather than repeating an uncorroborated exact thesis year; retained the switching-circuit connection. Boole’s publication dates and Karnaugh’s 1953 Bell Labs attribution were independently corroborated.
- Corrected RISC-V source labels that claimed to be MIT notes; added directly relevant primary Boole, Karnaugh and BOOM references.
- Coordinated with the different Data Representation verifier to broaden the earlier-owned winning register definition and prevent hardware-register phrases from opening a device-controller status-register entry.

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- RELRO protects the linker-defined GNU_RELRO range, not every relocated writable variable; eager binding protects applicable function slots.
- Memory-model glossary now covers both hardware ordering and source-language data-race rules. Generic atomics aliases no longer incorrectly imply every atomic operation is a read-modify-write.
- Register-class counting is ABI-specific: Windows shares positions while ordinary System V/AAPCS64 scalar classes are allocated separately; RDX upper-half return wording now specifically means 128-bit integers.
- Fixed-width LEA/shift arithmetic is explicitly bounded; the toy sum card now handles zero as an empty sum.
- Two question closing fences had swallowed important assumptions; they now render on their own lines and the actual mobile renderer checks their visibility.
- Readelf headers and checksec show binary indicators, not proof that every runtime mitigation is enforced. Canary stack placement, ASLR load addresses and alignment faults now have correct conditional wording.

## architecture: Caches & the Memory Hierarchy

[Independent verification record](../content/architecture/05-memory-hierarchy/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Uniform random cache access is now a fully occupied independent-access model and a poor-locality example, rather than a universal worst-case bound; adversarial conflicts can be worse.
- Column-major reuse-distance explanation now counts 1,023 intervening lines in a cycle of 1,024, rather than 1,024 other lines.
- The hypothetical cache-capacity staircase is explicitly schematic; its labels are not advertised as processor measurements.
- Address-bit and tile symbols now have standalone local context; organisation-table algebra is expressed in words, substitution units and grouping are explicit, and symbolic products use conventional unambiguous notation.
- One inherited question had closing formula-fence text on the same line; its closing fence now stands alone.
- The AMAT trade-off question’s unchanged winning key is retained, while the 6.12-cycle option correctly explains that the one-cycle saving more than pays for extra misses yet still loses to 5.6 cycles.
- HotSpot contiguous-array assumptions are distinguished from Java language semantics; CPython reference storage, shared objects and NumPy strides/views are kept distinct. array.array is taught locally before its associated question.
- Every original executable statement is unchanged; comments explain reference-array traversal, bottom-up AMAT, matrix reuse, tile origins, distinct-thread writes, page allocation assumptions and prefetch bounds.
- First-touch, anonymous-page, working-set, cold-cache and traffic glossary examples now carry their residency, fresh-page, policy and interference assumptions.
- Random replacement no longer claims no pathological trace or zero state cost; RRIP scan resistance is qualified as workload-dependent, and capacity/conflict misses explicitly use a fully associative LRU reference.
- Dirty-data preservation and eviction definitions allow buffered overlap/handover; streaming/non-temporal stores are instruction/configuration-dependent, and non-inclusive is no longer aliased as necessarily NINE.
- Relaxed atomics retain per-object ordering/coherence guarantees; release/acquire synchronisation depends on the acquire reading the matching release sequence. Hardware coherence does not make language data races valid.
- Test-and-set and test-and-test-and-set now resolve to different definitions. std::list is separated from std::vector; numastat is separated from numactl.
- Go is narrowly “Go programming language”/“Golang”, leaving ordinary “go” unlinked. Hardware prose uses coherence transactions rather than the database transaction fallback, and DRAM row buffer rather than a missing bare singular alias.
- MESIF Forward now means a designated clean responder when present; the text does not imply a Forward peer must always remain or answer every request. Textbook BusRd/MESI scope is explicit.
- NUMA nodes are distinguished from sockets, adjacent-line prefetching does not redefine the coherence granule, and 64/128-byte padding remains an explicit model or documented library heuristic.
- Inherited glossary definition/source review also qualifies ABI-specific binary32/binary64 language naming, matrix multiplication’s conventional cubic algorithm, burst modes, row-command timing and on-die ECC versus system ECC.
- Ordinary emphasis phrases and numeric answers no longer masquerade as new glossary terms; genuine technical terms and every substantive original drawing remain available.
- Independent review replaced an ambiguous false distractor: serialising AtomicLong through a lock can change active contention; the revised false option instead proposes unsafe unsynchronised shared increments. The original correct indices remain unchanged.
- Independent review corrected the 4,096-element column walk to 4,095 intervening lines, removed a stale reference to three consumers from a one-reader coherence example, and corrected the L3-service-time distractor description.
- Independent review distinguishes inclusive presence from up-to-date lower data, full versus partial write combining, current-level participation in global miss-rate products, comparator implementation choices and peer-cache line fills.
- Independent review qualifies RRIP victim predictions, columnar/SoA/array-padding benefit claims, inlining code-size effects, controller scope and memory-bound bottlenecks, and removes the overly broad AMBA-to-CHI alias.
- Independent review restored units/grouping to worked numeric substitutions and defined diagram tile labels before their preserved drawing; fixed stale split references.

Remaining gaps:

- No vendor-by-vendor cache geometry, replacement/inclusion-policy or cache/DRAM latency catalogue; the inherited explicit exclusions are retained rather than replacing them with guessed representative numbers.
- No normative/full CHI protocol, transient-state MESI implementation or coherence-liveness proof; this material teaches stable-state intuition before the later parallel hardware module.
- No claim of a measured matrix, false-sharing, NUMA or prefetch speedup on the user’s phone. Native outputs and bounded models are distinguished from hypothetical time/bandwidth inputs.
- C code fragments assume valid allocations/dimensions as documented; full allocation-error, overflow and tile-edge handling belong to their complete caller.
- No controlled physical multicore cache/NUMA/prefetch benchmark; native outputs and abstract model results establish only their stated environments and assumptions.
- Arm public overview plus primary gem5 implementation documentation support CHI orientation; no normative protocol conformance claim.

## architecture: Parallel Hardware

[Independent verification record](../content/architecture/06-parallelism/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Corrected the TPU weight-bandwidth substitution: byte bandwidth is multiplied by operations per byte, rather than being relabelled operations per second. All original answer keys remain unchanged.
- Corrected CUDA thread-block clusters to the documented co-scheduling guarantee within one GPU Processing Cluster; neighbouring physical SMs are not the API contract.
- Narrowed DRF-SC and Peterson definitions to the required language/machine-ordering assumptions; publication guarantees the value if observed, not scheduler fairness or bounded termination.
- XMM/YMM/ZMM now open an explicit 128/256/512-bit register-view definition. GCC -fassociative-math has its own qualified entry rather than aliasing the whole fast-math bundle.
- Separated sector-byte utilisation from execution-resource utilisation and core/memory-bound top-down subcategories from backend-bound; bad-speculation includes recovery slots.
- Replaced future-only Intel reference sources that omitted basic VADDPS/YMM coverage with the actual Intel basic architecture SDM; used specific Arm Neon and AMD DSP/timing primary manuals.
- Whole rendering caught opposite-sense contiguous-memory linking in a Non-contiguous heading and a proven-minimum Theory definition on a measured lower-bound estimate. Precise owned wording now avoids both.
- Fixed exact-equality signs on rounded Karp–Flatt, ceiling and floor intermediates; preserved 12 executable and 13 drawing/output body fences and all original 72 question contracts.

Remaining gaps:

- No CUDA-capable GPU, FPGA or TPU hardware is available. CUDA indexing and divergence fragments were exercised only through labelled CPU simulations; launch syntax/runtime synchronisation and actual device performance were not executed.
- Linux perf 6.12.111 installed in an ephemeral container. Real stat and record attempts fail with existing perf_event_open EPERM; report correctly sees no data. No permissions were changed. Displayed counter numbers and timing diagrams are illustrative, never measurements from this host.
- No physical x86 machine memory-order experiment was performed. AVX compiled x86-64 code ran through Rosetta on an arm64 host. Finite SC/TSO models and fetched ISA/language primary contracts support the ordering teaching; they are not exhaustive proofs of every concurrent program.
- Existing scope exclusions are retained: generic latency/energy/cost ratios, exhaustive MISD products and hardware-generation configuration catalogues, Rosetta implementation/performance attribution and universal Linux spinlock/PMU resource catalogues.
- The independent verifier reran exact CPU examples and wrote additional finite models; this does not provide CUDA launch execution, physical-GPU timing, FPGA timing closure, TPU execution or unrestricted PMU measurements. Existing perf EPERM was respected; no permission change or retry was attempted.

## languages: Programming Paradigms

[Independent verification record](../content/languages/01-paradigms/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Corrected quiz references that opened unrelated language pages, including HotSpot, spreadsheets, Bash scope, Datalog, JavaScript arrays and Haskell evaluation.
- Moved the Kubernetes manifest-recognition question into desired-state teaching, preserving all question identities and answer contracts.
- Corrected Java sealed declarations to restrict direct branches rather than all descendants; non-sealed branches may grow.
- Corrected GHC rewrite-rule/fusion claims: the compiler trusts rules and transformations must preserve termination/evaluation semantics even for pure code.
- Qualified Datalog termination to fair positive finite-domain bottom-up set evaluation; recursive CTEs and unrestricted dialects need their own stopping conditions.
- Corrected historical release/development distinctions for Elixir, F# and Erlang; removed the subjective friendlier-syntax claim and unsupported precise Curry biographical dates.
- Separated Go class-inheritance absence from interface embedding, inherited implementation from behavioral substitutability, and SCADE/Lustre clocked semantics from fixed-rate actor dataflow.
- Fixed a closing formula fence that shared its line with prose, and qualified closure retention/GC/frame allocation and parallelism implementation assumptions.

Remaining gaps:

- Advanced monads, persistent-structure implementation, type theory and compiler internals remain in their later course modules as in the original scope.
- No production cloud reconciliation, exhaustive engine survey, certification assessment or compiler performance benchmark; corresponding statements are qualified.
- A small Datalog unit is intentionally shorter; it includes the previously implicit synchronous-round trace required by its questions.
- No native ALGOL/CLU or Safari execution; no live Kubernetes/Terraform/cloud deployment, compiler-IR/disassembly survey or production performance benchmark.
- Original depth and all 64 executable statement sequences preserved; examples remain educational fixtures rather than production banking/concurrency/deployment implementations.

Low-confidence areas:

- **medium** — para-state-and-control: Paradigm boundaries and how/what taxonomy. Pedagogical categories rather than a universal standard; operational examples and control-flow theorem checked against primary sources.
- **medium** — para-polymorphic-dispatch: HotSpot guarded inlining and runtime dispatch costs. Official implementation glossary checked; Java outputs executed, but no compiler-IR/disassembly or performance benchmark. Exact optimisations remain version/workload dependent.
- **medium** — para-recursion-costs: Proper-tail-call engine support and default recursion limit. Primary WebKit and Python documentation plus native Node/CPython behavior; no Safari runtime or exhaustive engine survey. Limits/configuration and engine support can change.
- **medium** — para-choosing-language-style: Language/team/ecosystem and optimisation guidance. Rules of thumb and workload-dependent opportunities, not measured productivity rankings or unconditional speed guarantees.
- **medium** — para-closure-storage: Closure physical storage, GC and upvalue details. Primary CPython/Lua/Rust/C++ sources and native fixtures support the examples; allocation can change under optimisation and unused bindings need not be retained.
- **medium** — para-nonstrict-arguments: ALGOL call-by-name and CLU argument-sharing attribution. ALGOL original report section 4.7.3.2 extracted from the Computer History Museum preservation scan. CLU TR190 primary indexed excerpt independently retrieved, but local scan is image-only. Native ALGOL/CLU execution was not available; abstract demand models and Python mutation/rebinding checked.
