## languages: Memory Management & Garbage Collection

[Independent verification record](../content/languages/03-memory-management/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Corrected tool coverage: standalone LeakSanitizer can be linked without access instrumentation, while ASan interposition can detect some external-library faults without comprehensively checking an arbitrary existing binary.
- Qualified JVM reference-width assumptions, fixed-size stack fast paths/cache claims, stack-overflow failure guarantees, and physical placement versus the language model.
- Corrected freed-pointer teaching: C pointer bits are not automatically set to NULL, but pointers to an ended object become indeterminate; expired-object access is undefined independently of allocator reuse.
- Corrected Go blocked-goroutine retention to values needed by the suspended computation, GC eligibility versus prompt collection, and fork page copying to ordinary private mappings rather than every mapping.
- Qualified G1 defaults to configuration, region selection to predicted costs and eligibility, card processing to refinement policy, and repeated self-healing across collector phases.
- Corrected Arc payload Send/Sync qualifications, final strong release versus weak bookkeeping storage, Rust reference lifetimes versus storage extension, safe reborrowing, and moves versus physical stack copies.
- Added direct original McCarthy and Cheney papers; distinguished the usual general-object Cheney presentation from the original list-structure paper, and scoped semispace memory doubling to its two equal managed spaces.
- Qualified eager-count release cascades, weak-reference APIs, deferred-count reconciliation, sync.Pool retention, percentile ties and collector recommendations; kept all original questions and executable statements.

Remaining gaps:

- No original teaching depth, program example, diagram or question removed; sources and final caveats accompany the shorter units.
- Production collector performance and physical JIT allocation elimination were not benchmarked; descriptions are sourced mechanisms and design goals, never workload guarantees.
- Native tests use the stated compiler/runtime versions and Linux/macOS ARM64 targets; they are not universal ABI or every-version proofs.
- Phone checks use the actual production renderer/CSS/dictionary/diagram compiler in an isolated 390 px harness; full app-route, completion-date migration, PWA/offline and publication checks belong to coordinating integration after independent verification.
- All 51 active typed program fragments independently rerun through the author's transparent external wrappers (79 checks), plus four independently written native fixtures; sanitizer failures are expected diagnostics, not successful undefined-behaviour output guarantees.
- Independent finite graph/arithmetic models do not establish correctness of production concurrent collectors. No actual cgroup configuration changes, JIT IR/disassembly, production collector benchmark, live long-running memory-pressure test or physical phone performance measurement.
- Historical McCarthy/Cheney original papers independently fetched; deferred-counting explanation corroborated by accessible original-author research rather than inaccessible ACM-hosted PDFs. No claim of independently reproducing original historic implementations.

- Made safe-publication examples explicitly Java-specific: a volatile field is not a universal language-independent synchronisation rule.
- Corrected remaining glossary assumptions about Haskell Int and generic 64-bit pointers, JavaScript recursion error types, enumerable object spread, and compiler/runtime-dependent tail support.
- Strengthened the well-founded-measure definition with finite recursive branching and terminating nonrecursive work; corrected the naive Fibonacci tree claim that every level branches twice.
- Aligned persistent-structure and HAMT wording with actual implementations: bitmap entries can be key/value pairs or children; balanced trees do not require every leaf at nearly the same depth; memoised work must actually be shared.
- Scoped immutable identity/change detection, container-functor intuition, async/await behavior, Spark metadata/setup work and F# builder-based computation expressions to their actual contracts.
- Replaced 23 unrelated question reading links with primary sources for the actual JavaScript, Haskell, Python, Rust or HAMT mechanism being tested.

- The original module teaches practical monad patterns, not a full category-theory development or general proofs for partial/effectful programs.

- No arbitrary-program functor/monad proof, portable recursion threshold or production benchmark is claimed. The lazy ten-million fold remains a bounded incomplete execution; strict counterpart and finite forcing/law models pass.

## languages: Compilers & Interpreters

[Independent verification record](../content/languages/05-compilers/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- AOT can use runtime profiles collected earlier; PGO is not exclusively an AOT-only activity.
- Rust regex is a third-party crate, not the Rust standard library; single-search and repeated-search bounds differ.
- A C lvalue need not be modifiable; a const-qualified object can still be an lvalue.
- Left factoring does not guarantee the entire grammar LL(1); FIRST/empty-alternative analysis also needs following tokens.
- Not every AST removes parentheses, and token-based parsing is not the only parsing architecture.
- SSA is static single assignment with available definitions, not single execution; LLVM phi placement, loop headers and incoming-edge semantics qualified.
- C signed left shift requires a nonnegative left operand, even when a negative multiplication result would fit.
- JVM max_stack counts long/double values as two units, unlike the one-value Calc model.
- Register-allocation/coalescing and live-interval claims now state interchangeable-register, equal-value, ABI and side-effect assumptions.
- W^X does not imply every JIT must use one specific permission-switching technique; runtime deoptimisation destinations and performance costs vary.
- Geometric mean does not automatically neutralise outliers; cache misses may hit another cache rather than main memory.
- Swift’s parser is no longer incorrectly included in a universal precedence-climbing claim; TypeScript parser evidence is versioned.
- The branch label in the SSA drawing now agrees with its C/LLVM example.
- FIRST now distinguishes deriving the empty string from deriving no string; Pratt and recursive-descent definitions no longer overstate equivalence or require hand writing.
- The immediate-operand example was invalid as a real AArch64 multiply-immediate; it is now a genuine assembled add-immediate example. LEA tiling distinguishes base/index registers from a constant displacement.
- CIL execution can use JIT or AOT; callee-saved ABI wording includes the stack pointer and conservative coalescing preserves colourability rather than promising an easier problem.
- A parser-practice question requiring the production-parser examples moved after those examples, preserving its ID, key, option count, level and type. The polymorphic-cache question now has explicit local teaching support.

## languages: Concurrency Models in Languages

[Independent verification record](../content/languages/06-concurrency-models/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Bounded concurrency is not bounded retained memory: semaphore waiters still occupy task state; the glossary now agrees with the lesson’s bounded-backlog warning.
- Actor timeout observations alone do not prove a cyclic wait. The question now gives an explicit two-handler wait trace, and the asynchronous fix explicitly returns to the receive loop.
- Erlang selective receive removes the first matching message in queue order, which OTP priority insertion can distinguish from chronological arrival. Priority links and monitors are included alongside explicit alias sends.
- Parking an OS thread consumes that thread resource; parking a user-level task can free its carrier. M:1 blocking wording now distinguishes supported cooperative operations from unsupported native calls.
- Java Future does not expose continuation callbacks; the glossary distinguishes it from CompletableFuture/CompletionStage and Scala Future.
- Corrected comments on the locked counter and released Rust guard so they describe the fixed operation; replaced stale whole-lesson numbers with descriptive short-unit references.
- Data races need not occur at the same physical instant. Rust borrowing text now uses conflicting accesses and required ordering.
- JavaScript ordinary objects and all Promises are truthy; the legacy browser document.all exception prevents an inaccurate universal-object definition.
- Removed exact duplicate Further reading URLs while retaining each distinct source and all substantive lesson material.
- Historical async feature chronology is sourced; an unmeasured popularity claim was removed.

Remaining gaps:

- Historical Solaris native M:N, old Go 1.13 cooperative pre-emption, JDK 21 monitor pinning and OTP 28 priority insertion were checked against primary artifacts/docs, not emulated on all named releases.
- Native tests use explicit drivers/imports/stubs for partial snippets and named runtimes. No production speed, all-schedule fairness, starvation freedom, arbitrary memory capacity or formal liveness proof is claimed.

- Clarified collision/tag forgery guarantees and shared-key attribution; MAC and MACs now resolve to the same earlier owned precise definition.

- Corrected mechanical mandatory-access-control/network-address distractor and residual uncarded entropy expression.

- The TOCTOU C fragment was compiled as an ordinary object only; no setuid binary or privilege-race exploit was deployed or executed.
- Password-search rates and additive attack costs are explicit hypothetical models. Native assertions verify only finite cases; no attacker-performance benchmark, constant-time timing proof or intrusion exercise is supplied.

## security: Cryptography Basics

[Independent verification record](../content/security/02-crypto-basics/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Repaired three closing formula fences that shared a line with following prose; all 121 cards now compile and phone rendering is clean.
- Corrected a false equality between an unreduced Diffie–Hellman power and its remainder; independently checked all toy modular and curve values and both revised drawing directions/values.
- Corrected Ed25519 private signing scalar versus per-message secret nonce derivation against RFC 8032; RFC native vector passes.
- Removed the unsupported Pollard-rho constant range and retained a variant-dependent expected square-root work model. Qualified heuristic GNFS and idealised search/time estimates.
- Replaced absolute AEAD, MAC and OAEP guarantees with the scheme’s assumptions, encoding checks and forgery bounds; clarified that OAEP does not authenticate a sender.
- Separated non-coprime from coprime, NaCl from libsodium, OpenPGP from GnuPG, Kyber from ML-KEM, IoT from embedded devices, and Keccak from SHA-3 with six precise glossary entries.
- Removed broad aliases for keying material, RSA padding, generic TLS handshakes and EdDSA JWT; compiled probes verify all 248 canonical terms and 690 alias senses, including earlier-owned definitions.
- Corrected SHA-3/truncated state, ambiguous-input encoding, RSA two-prime versus multi-prime, subgroup, KDF, key separation, operating-system RNG and platform-specific password-library claims.
- Qualified the historical Chrome 131 statement to the September 2024 announced transition, which the original Google source actually establishes.
- Moved ECC-strength questions after scalar-security teaching, entropy questions to randomness, and direction-label derivation questions to HKDF. Replaced generic print comments with descriptions of actual results.

Remaining gaps:

- Formal HMAC security reductions and complete asymptotic attack proofs are not taught; constructions and assumptions are checked against standards and original-author cryptography references.
- No hardware throughput benchmarks, resource-backed quantum attack estimate or proof that a toy implementation is secure is supplied. Hypothetical rates and generic work models are explicitly conditional.
- Demonstrations omit complete production authentication, provisioning, replay protection, storage, rotation and operational error handling. Library boundaries and per-protocol requirements remain necessary.
- Original 2004 MD5 collision paper endpoints returned HTTP 403 in this environment. The historical date is supported by later original research and retained at medium confidence; no direct access to that original paper is claimed.
- Generic mathematical definitions use original-author Handbook of Applied Cryptography chapters as authoritative references, supplemented by native checks and standards; not every basic definition has a separate first-publication source.
- This is a subdivision of existing material, not a complete course in modern cryptographic protocol proofs or post-quantum migration.

## security: Applied Cryptography & PKI

[Independent verification record](../content/security/03-applied-crypto/verification.json)

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Later TLS HKDF salt inputs can contain derived secrets; generic public-salt terminology cannot describe every TLS stage. Raw ECDH material is not itself a set of independent traffic keys.
- Use current RFC9846 (July2026), which obsoletes8446, while preserving the published8448 execution vector. Scope classical ECDHE examples and PSK mode terminology correctly.
- Qualify EUF-CMA as a computational/negligible-probability goal, distinguish signing authority/private key from a public identity prekey, and make ECDSA distinct-message nonce reuse conditions explicit.
- Separate generic secret RNG requirements from permitted deterministic unique GCM nonces. Distinguish explicit NIST invocation policy from merely solving the birthday approximation.
- Use original HMAC key K separately from its processed block K_0; verify long-key hashing/padding natively.
- Clarify revocation versus mathematical signature validity, planned versus compromise KEK rotation, and recovery requiring fresh DEKs when exposure is possible.
- Do not map TLS traffic secrets to actual AEAD keys, public ratchet keys to key pairs, KMS services to KMS-key resources, skipped ciphertexts to skipped message keys, IP protocol to IP addresses, or issuer parties to issuer certificate objects.
- Constrain balanced-tree formula to maximum path depth; actual MLS update work also depends on populated state/resolutions.
- Remove unconditional HSM tamper/side-channel guarantees and legal attribution promises; retain model/operational qualifications.
- Final live dictionary inspection exposed JSON/sorting/proof/CI definitions on object keys/key comparisons/derivation steps/lead time; owned wording now uses precise cryptographic meanings.

Remaining gaps:

- No jurisdiction-specific legal assessment of signature evidence, deniability, admissibility or GDPR erasure compliance. Existing explicit legal gaps preserved.
- No claimed general measurements for HSM throughput, KMS fleet latency, credential discovery times, provider outage frequency, or worldwide crypto failure proportions. Existing measurement gaps preserved.
- No live AWS/HSM/device/Signal/MLS interoperability or complete production protocol implementation; cloud API fragment uses a clearly recorded local shape fixture.
- FIPS140-3 references its normative ISO requirements; removed the inherited unverified level-specific summary rather than claiming an inaccessible detailed audit.
- No physical quantum implementation or quantum arrival forecast; Grover counts are idealised planning examples, and current products/standards policies are date-sensitive.
- Ancillary glossary definitions with secondary or landing-page-only provenance retain individually listed medium confidence; automated word matches are diagnostics rather than evidence of factual proof.
- Primary TLS review uses RFC9846 (July2026), which obsoletes RFC8446; RFC8448 remains the original published execution-vector source.
- The isolated release starts at ce1d90cb72d707f0bea7a03115c0c1daaa6ac93b, with only this module overlaid; independently published Cryptography supplies earlier prerequisites.

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Preflight caching does not require identical credentials modes: an include-enabled entry can cover non-include requests, while the reverse is excluded. Changed headers can reuse permissions already granted. Added network-partition matching and tested both directions/header grants in real local Chrome.

The independently checked CSP3 strict-dynamic algorithm does allow eligible non-parser-inserted inline text while eval/string timers/handlers remain separately blocked.

- {"id":"sort-hash-operation","before":{"id":"sort-hash-operation","term":"Sort and hash operations","aliases":["sort and hash operations","sort or hash operation","hash-based operations"],"definition":"Query plan steps such as sorts, hash joins and hash aggregation that need working memory. A sort has a work_mem base allowance; hash operations use work_mem times hash_mem_multiplier, and multiple operations or parallel workers can multiply consumption.","example":"A query that hash-joins two tables and then sorts the result runs two such operations at once.","source":"https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/runtime-config-resource.html"},"after":{"id":"sort-hash-operation","term":"Sort and hash operations","aliases":["sort and hash operations","sort or hash operation","hash-based operations"],"definition":"Query plan steps such as sorts, hash joins and hash aggregation that need working memory. A sort has a work_mem base allowance; hash operations use work_mem times hash_mem_multiplier, and multiple operations or parallel workers can multiply consumption.","example":"A hash join followed by a sort uses working-memory plan operations; which allocations overlap and their sizes depend on the execution plan.","source":"https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/runtime-config-resource.html"}}
- {"id":"autovacuum-freeze-max-age","before":{"id":"autovacuum-freeze-max-age","term":"Maximum XID age for autovacuum","aliases":["autovacuum_freeze_max_age"],"definition":"The XID age at which PostgreSQL forces an anti-wraparound vacuum of a table, even if autovacuum is disabled. Default 200 million transactions.","example":"200 million is under a tenth of the roughly 2.1 billion XID comparison horizon.","source":"https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/runtime-config-vacuum.html"},"after":{"id":"autovacuum-freeze-max-age","term":"Maximum XID age for autovacuum","aliases":["autovacuum_freeze_max_age"],"definition":"The allocated-XID age that a table's oldest-unfrozen-XID boundary can reach before PostgreSQL forces an anti-wraparound vacuum, even if ordinary autovacuum is disabled. The PostgreSQL 18 default is 200 million allocated transaction IDs; this is not elapsed time or a count of committed top-level transactions.","example":"200 million is under a tenth of the roughly 2.1 billion XID comparison horizon.","source":"https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/runtime-config-vacuum.html"}}
- {"id":"xid-wraparound","before":{"id":"xid-wraparound","term":"Transaction ID wraparound","aliases":["wraparound","XID wraparound","wraparound risk"],"definition":"The failure that would occur when the 32-bit XID counter cycles around: old rows would appear to be in the future and vanish. VACUUM prevents it by freezing old row versions before they are about 2 billion transactions old.","example":"A server doing 1,000 writing transactions a second uses 2 billion XIDs in about 23 days.","source":"https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/routine-vacuuming.html"},"after":{"id":"xid-wraparound","term":"Transaction ID wraparound","aliases":["wraparound","XID wraparound","wraparound risk"],"definition":"The visibility failure that old unfrozen committed insertions could suffer when normal 32-bit XIDs advance beyond their approximately half-range safe ordering window. Freezing protects those old insertions before they become too old for normal comparisons.","example":"At exactly 1,000 allocated normal XIDs per second, two billion IDs take about 23 days; subtransactions can increase ID consumption beyond a top-level transaction rate.","source":"https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/routine-vacuuming.html"}}
- {"id":"connection-limit","before":{"id":"connection-limit","term":"Connection limit","aliases":["connection limits","max_connections"],"definition":"The maximum number of concurrent connections allowed. The default three superuser-reserved slots and any additional reserved_connections slots reduce the number available to an ordinary role.","source":"https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/runtime-config-connection.html"}}

## synth: Sound & Digital Audio

[Independent verification record](https://github.com/AlfieGormley/cs-study-app/blob/af9e91cd29ba790780489a8f1d6a2da6f4151119/content/synth/01-sound-sampling/verification.json)

A scoped follow-up is underway. This section describes the previously published version.

Corrections and qualifications in this conversion:

- Moved snd-waves-q8 from wave measurements to sample-phase increment so its sampled-sine and samples-per-cycle prerequisites precede it; original ID and key retained.
- Added a local changing-frequency/absolute-time explanation and formula to wrapped-phase state before snd-waves-q11; its 44,880 Hz result independently recomputed.
- Phase preservation avoids an output-value jump but does not guarantee no transient; abrupt slope changes and finite rounding now explained in lesson and questions.
- Float headroom avoids clipping but is not lossless arithmetic; normal rounding still applies.
- Full relative float precision restricted to the normal range; subnormal precision differs.
- Digital full scale distinguished from the representable float range in dBFS prose, formula definition and question explanation.
- TPDF output error has input-independent mean and variance; removed the stronger claim that every property of nonsubtractive total error is statistically independent.
- Specified common TPDF dither as two quantisation steps peak-to-peak, including the moved dither question.
- Distinguished formal SINAD-based ENOB from the question’s explicitly requested noise-only estimate from dynamic range; added verified SINAD glossary definition.
- Separated BLIT impulse-train integration from BLEP/PolyBLEP step correction.
- FM described as generating sidebands, which need not be harmonics.
- Oversampling decimation filtering removes out-of-passband content; in-band aliases created at the oversampled rate survive.
- High-rate hard-clipping experiment greatly reduces alias contamination; finite rate cannot remove the infinite harmonic tail.
- Odd-only symmetric clipping statement restricted to a sine or other input with half-wave symmetry.
- Added DC/Nyquist endpoint exceptions to the first FFT scaling description, and excluded these bins explicitly in the amplitude question.
- Rectangular window allowed for coherent whole-cycle captures; removed universal “always window” instruction.
- Added local zero-padding and scalloping/flat-top/interpolation context before FFT-bin and leakage questions.
- DC question now specifies whole cycles of a steady waveform; lesson distinguishes partial-cycle mean from genuine DC offset.
- Full-scale sample counts labelled as warnings for investigation, not proof that clipping happened, in WAV checklist and moved questions.
- Eight-bit nearest-rounding question now explicitly excludes endpoint overload, retaining the same key.
- Block-budget question now asks about meeting each block’s deadline, rather than an average which could conceal overruns.
- Quantiser trace table reduced from five to four columns with all intermediate round/clip values retained.
- Rejected new bare fixed-point alias because current hyphenated fallback could capture mathematical fixed points in other subjects; retained precise arithmetic aliases.
- Rejected new bare phase alias because it would capture envelope “release phase” in another Synth module; used existing precise wave-phase phrase locally.
- Fractional/negative powers no longer described as fractional numbers of repeated copies; roots and reciprocals made explicit.
- Replaced obsolete original lesson-number cross-references with relevant short-unit names.
- Bounded phase avoids growing-accumulator resolution loss but floating-point arithmetic still rounds.
- The 100-cent equal-ratio semitone is explicitly 12-TET; other tunings have other semitone sizes and smaller intervals.
- Wavetable is a general stored waveform; spectral restriction is deliberate, and interpolation/playback affect aliasing. Old Band-limited wavetable title retained as alias.

Remaining gaps:

- No original course topic removed: 68 original questions/keys, 51 original lesson equations, 20 Python lesson examples, three diagrams and two flow diagrams retained; one new sine question and one local glide formula added.
- The 295 existing glossary definitions and confidence records are inherited from prior independent verification, not claimed as a fresh exhaustive definition audit. Real source limitations are retained; ENOB/JND/phase-jump/peak-level shorthand is explicitly flagged above.
- Primary sources, formulas, all question keys/distractors and all code reviewed; claimsChecked counts 220 overlapping review units (32 lesson claim groups + 112 formula blocks + 69 question reviews + six requested alias-entry reviews + one new glossary definition), not 220 independently enumerated atomic claims.
- Offline examples and exact outputs were tested, including PCM conversion boundaries. Hardware, audible quality, embedded callback timing, board noise and processor-specific slow paths were not measured.
- BLIT/BLEP, oversampling filters and true-peak reconstruction are conceptual here; implementation depth remains in the later synthesis/audio-engineering modules.
- Repository-wide formula/glossary scans also enumerate preserved archival files; their unchanged old lint forms are out of the active short-lesson editing scope. All active formula-lint hits are real inline program code permitted by Rule 6.
- Complete app build, all-app tests and 390 px browser rendering are assigned to the coordinating session; this verifier does not modify generated data or shared compiler/app files.
- AES Pro Audio Reference was fetched with the web browser for evidence, but the final Node accessibility check returned HTTP 403. Its newly added Further reading link was removed; evidence URL remains recorded as a browser-fetched source.

Low-confidence areas:

- **medium** — glossary: coherent-sampling. No dedicated authoritative page fetched (Analog Devices article timed out); source is the Spectral leakage article
- **medium** — glossary: note-space. Course-specific term; Pitch space article supports the linear MIDI pitch-space idea
- **medium** — glossary: crest-factor. "12–20 dB for real music" is a broad typical range that varies by genre and mastering
- **medium** — glossary: zipper-noise. Source (Smoothing) is generic; definition is standard practice
- **medium** — glossary: parameter-smoothing. Source (Smoothing) is generic; definition is standard practice
- **medium** — glossary: offline-render. Source is a computer-graphics rendering article; meaning is standard audio usage
- **medium** — glossary: correlated-signals. Source (Coherence) is loose; power-addition of uncorrelated signals is standard
- **medium** — glossary: power-signal. Source (Power (physics)) is loose; mean-square definition is standard
- **medium** — glossary: vibrato. Example settings are a typical choice, not a standard
- **medium** — glossary: monitor-level. Alias "studio monitors" names the speakers rather than the level
- **medium** — glossary: audio-block. Course usage; PortAudio callback docs describe frames per buffer
- **medium** — glossary: ableton-live. Existing medium-confidence glossary record preserved. Current manufacturer manual documents C-2 to G8 full MIDI range, from which note 60 = C3 is inferred; product labels remain version-sensitive.
- **medium** — glossary: enob. Existing definition and original confidence retained unchanged as requested. Its SNR/dynamic-range shortcut describes noise-only resolution; formal ENOB uses SINAD. Active lesson/question explicitly distinguish this.
- **medium** — glossary: just-noticeable-difference. Existing definition and original confidence retained unchanged. Its 3 Hz/0.6% examples come from a secondary summary; primary research supports condition dependence, not a universal threshold.
- **medium** — glossary: phase-jump and peak-level. Existing definitions and confidences preserved. They simplify discontinuity and clipping conditions; active lessons now specify possible specially timed continuous phase changes and sample-peak versus reconstructed-peak limits.
- **medium** — snd-cents-beating: Pitch discrimination thresholds and fast-beat perceptual boundaries. Research and UNSW demonstrations support dependence on stimulus/task/listener; no universal numerical threshold established. Lesson retains only explicitly hedged secondary examples.
- **medium** — snd-midi-frequency: Ableton note-60 display label. C3 is inferred from the current manual’s full MIDI range C-2 to G8; future versions/products may use other octave labels.
- **medium** — snd-float-integer-boundary: Target-processor subnormal handling cost. Primary Intel documentation and older x86 measurements support variable penalties. No benchmark on the reader’s embedded target was performed; fixed-cycle claim removed.
- **medium** — snd-dither-converter-noise: Finished-board dynamic range and noise-only ENOB estimate. Chip figures verified with datasheet configurations and A-weighting; finished-board noise and SINAD were not measured. Dynamic-range substitution remains an explicitly approximate noise-only estimate.
- **medium** — snd-peak-rms-dbfs: AES17 convention and typical music crest-factor ranges. AES authoritative reference and indexed Audio Precision primary-manual excerpt support the conventions; full normative AES17 text not obtained. Music range is programme- and averaging-dependent and is hedged.
- **medium** — snd-wav-write-readback: PCM flooring/clipping and library conversion behaviour. Confirmed in independent experiments with soundfile 0.14.0/libsndfile 1.2.2 and inspected versioned source. Behaviour can depend on library/platform/rounding configuration; text ties it to this runtime.
